People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Well, I came in because my head just feels… cloudy. Like at work, I’ll be looking at a board packet or some vendor email, and I just sit there staring at it. I used to make those little decisions fast, you know, like which thing needs signing or who to call back, and now even simple things feel hard to sort out.  

And it’s funny, because I’ve been doing property stuff a long time—people think it’s just keys and complaints, but it’s a lot of papers—and lately I just can’t get my mind to click into place.

Yeah… I guess I haven’t been enjoying them like I used to. Like my dance class—I used to look forward to that, even if I wasn’t the best one there, you know, I liked seeing the ladies and moving around a little. Lately I just don’t want to go.

Some things, not so much lately. I used to like going to my dance class, even if I wasn’t the best one there, you know, those ladies can really move. And I liked walking around those little galleries, just looking, not buying anything.

I don’t know… tired, yes, sometimes, but I’m not sure if it’s the kind you mean. I’m 64, so some days I sit down and think, well, maybe that’s just my body telling me to slow down.

Not really. \n\nIt’s more like I’ll sit there and feel stuck, and then I start thinking about all the paperwork piling up, and then that reminds me of this one board president who used to call three times a day about the dumbest things… sorry, I’m getting off track. I guess I haven’t really noticed feeling sad, just… off.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things you used to like?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I haven’t been enjoying them like I used to. Like my dance class—I used to look forward to that, even if I wasn’t the best one there, you know, I liked seeing the ladies and moving around a little. Lately I just don’t want to go.\n\nAnd there are these little galleries I used to poke into when I had time, just to look around, not buy anything, because who has wall space anymore… but I haven’t felt like doing that either. Even cooking Sunday food for my family, which usually makes me feel like myself, I just… don’t really want to do it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really been watching the scale, to be honest. My eating’s been strange though. Like I’ll have coffee and maybe a few bites of something, and somehow that carries me most of the day.\n\nThen at night I’ll be standing in the kitchen picking at tortillas or some sweets, not even because I want them, just… they’re there. My mother used to say if you’re going to eat, sit down and eat properly, but I don’t really do that lately. I don’t know if my weight changed much, but my appetite’s definitely off.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: Sleep… I’m not sure. I mean, I go to bed, and some nights I’m looking at the ceiling thinking about things, but then again my neighbor’s dog starts up sometimes, and once that happens I’m listening for every little noise.\n\nI can’t really say if it’s a “sleep problem” or just me not settling down right. I haven’t been keeping track of it or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things that happen or things you've done?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do get that feeling. Mostly with the building, the residents. I keep thinking I talked too big, like I promised things before I really checked the money right, and now I feel like I let everybody down.\n\nNobody’s come and said, “You ruined it,” or anything like that, but I still keep turning it over in my head. It’s silly maybe, because you know how people complain about the hall lights or the gate and then next week it’s the parking spaces… but I still feel bad, like I should’ve known better.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy or find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Some things, not so much lately. I used to like going to my dance class, even if I wasn’t the best one there, you know, those ladies can really move. And I liked walking around those little galleries, just looking, not buying anything.\n\nAnd Sundays… I used to cook proper food for my family, the way my mother did, with enough for whoever showed up. Lately I just don’t feel like doing any of that. Those were the things that made me feel like myself, and I’ve kind of been avoiding them.\n\nDoctor: Are there any small pleasures in life that you still find enjoyable?\n\nPatient: Honestly, not many right now. I mean, I still notice little things, like if the coffee comes out good or the sun hits the kitchen in the morning, but the things I usually enjoy… I haven’t really wanted them.\n\nLike my dance class, I keep telling myself I’ll go next week, and then I don’t. And those little galleries I used to wander through, just to look at the paintings and the pottery and all that, I haven’t felt like doing it. Even Sunday cooking for my family… that used to make me feel like me, you know? Now it just feels like too much, and I end up not doing it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel tired or lacking in energy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… tired, yes, sometimes, but I’m not sure if it’s the kind you mean. I’m 64, so some days I sit down and think, well, maybe that’s just my body telling me to slow down.\n\nBut then other days I can get up and do things around the building if I have to. You know, there’s always something — someone saying the elevator smells funny, or the mailbox key isn’t right — so I do move around. I just don’t always feel like I have that… push. I’m not sure if that counts as low energy or just being worn out.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about “significant.” I haven’t really been checking the scale like that. My eating has been strange, though. Some days it’s just coffee and a few bites of something, and that carries me most of the day.\n\nThen later at night I’ll pick at tortillas or something sweet, but it’s not like I’m enjoying it much. It’s more just… there in the kitchen, and I take a little, then walk away. My mother would have told me that’s no way to eat, and she’d be right.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed your clothes fitting looser than they used to?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little, but I haven’t really been paying close attention to the clothes part. I wear a lot of loose things around the apartment anyway, you know, because I’m going up and down stairs or dealing with people in the building.\n\nBut my eating has definitely been off. Like I said, sometimes coffee and a few bites of something will carry me most of the day, and then at night I’ll pick at tortillas or something sweet, not because I’m really enjoying it, just because it’s there. So… maybe that would make things fit different, I’m just not sure.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Not really. \n\nIt’s more like I’ll sit there and feel stuck, and then I start thinking about all the paperwork piling up, and then that reminds me of this one board president who used to call three times a day about the dumbest things… sorry, I’m getting off track. I guess I haven’t really noticed feeling sad, just… off.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things you used to like?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I haven’t been enjoying them like I used to. Like my dance class—I used to look forward to that, even if I wasn’t the best one there, you know, I liked seeing the ladies and moving around a little. Lately I just don’t want to go.\n\nAnd there are these little galleries I used to poke into when I had time, just to look around, not buy anything, because who has wall space anymore… but I haven’t felt like doing that either. Even cooking Sunday food for my family, which usually makes me feel like myself, I just… don’t really want to do it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really been watching the scale, to be honest. My eating’s been strange though. Like I’ll have coffee and maybe a few bites of something, and somehow that carries me most of the day.\n\nThen at night I’ll be standing in the kitchen picking at tortillas or some sweets, not even because I want them, just… they’re there. My mother used to say if you’re going to eat, sit down and eat properly, but I don’t really do that lately. I don’t know if my weight changed much, but my appetite’s definitely off.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: Sleep… I’m not sure. I mean, I go to bed, and some nights I’m looking at the ceiling thinking about things, but then again my neighbor’s dog starts up sometimes, and once that happens I’m listening for every little noise.\n\nI can’t really say if it’s a “sleep problem” or just me not settling down right. I haven’t been keeping track of it or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things that happen or things you've done?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do get that feeling. Mostly with the building, the residents. I keep thinking I talked too big, like I promised things before I really checked the money right, and now I feel like I let everybody down.\n\nNobody’s come and said, “You ruined it,” or anything like that, but I still keep turning it over in my head. It’s silly maybe, because you know how people complain about the hall lights or the gate and then next week it’s the parking spaces… but I still feel bad, like I should’ve known better.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy or find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Some things, not so much lately. I used to like going to my dance class, even if I wasn’t the best one there, you know, those ladies can really move. And I liked walking around those little galleries, just looking, not buying anything.\n\nAnd Sundays… I used to cook proper food for my family, the way my mother did, with enough for whoever showed up. Lately I just don’t feel like doing any of that. Those were the things that made me feel like myself, and I’ve kind of been avoiding them.\n\nDoctor: Are there any small pleasures in life that you still find enjoyable?\n\nPatient: Honestly, not many right now. I mean, I still notice little things, like if the coffee comes out good or the sun hits the kitchen in the morning, but the things I usually enjoy… I haven’t really wanted them.\n\nLike my dance class, I keep telling myself I’ll go next week, and then I don’t. And those little galleries I used to wander through, just to look at the paintings and the pottery and all that, I haven’t felt like doing it. Even Sunday cooking for my family… that used to make me feel like me, you know? Now it just feels like too much, and I end up not doing it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel tired or lacking in energy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… tired, yes, sometimes, but I’m not sure if it’s the kind you mean. I’m 64, so some days I sit down and think, well, maybe that’s just my body telling me to slow down.\n\nBut then other days I can get up and do things around the building if I have to. You know, there’s always something — someone saying the elevator smells funny, or the mailbox key isn’t right — so I do move around. I just don’t always feel like I have that… push. I’m not sure if that counts as low energy or just being worn out.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about “significant.” I haven’t really been checking the scale like that. My eating has been strange, though. Some days it’s just coffee and a few bites of something, and that carries me most of the day.\n\nThen later at night I’ll pick at tortillas or something sweet, but it’s not like I’m enjoying it much. It’s more just… there in the kitchen, and I take a little, then walk away. My mother would have told me that’s no way to eat, and she’d be right.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed your clothes fitting looser than they used to?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little, but I haven’t really been paying close attention to the clothes part. I wear a lot of loose things around the apartment anyway, you know, because I’m going up and down stairs or dealing with people in the building.\n\nBut my eating has definitely been off. Like I said, sometimes coffee and a few bites of something will carry me most of the day, and then at night I’ll pick at tortillas or something sweet, not because I’m really enjoying it, just because it’s there. So… maybe that would make things fit different, I’m just not sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost more than 5% of your body weight in the past month?\n\nPatient: I couldn’t say 5%, doctor. I don’t really keep track like that, and the scale… half the time I forget it’s even there.\n\nI just know my eating’s been off. Like coffee and a few bites of something will get me through most of the day, and then at night I’ll pick at tortillas or something sweet, but I’m not really enjoying it. It’s more like I wander into the kitchen, take a little, then go back to whatever I was doing. So maybe I’ve lost some, but I don’t know the number.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n13\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.0, F32.1, F50.8\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I came in because my head just feels… cloudy. Like at work, I’ll be looking at a board packet or some vendor email, and I just sit there staring at it. I used to make those little decisions fast, you know, like which thing needs signing or who to call back, and now even simple things feel hard to sort out.  \n\nAnd it’s funny, because I’ve been doing property stuff a long time—people think it’s just keys and complaints, but it’s a lot of papers—and lately I just can’t get my mind to click into place.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: Sad… I don’t know if I’d call it that exactly. I mean, I get frustrated, sure, especially when I can’t keep up with things the way I used to. But sad like crying all day? Not really. \n\nIt’s more like I’ll sit there and feel stuck, and then I start thinking about all the paperwork piling up, and then that reminds me of this one board president who used to call three times a day about the dumbest things… sorry, I’m getting off track. I guess I haven’t really noticed feeling sad, just… off.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things you used to like?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I haven’t been enjoying them like I used to. Like my dance class—I used to look forward to that, even if I wasn’t the best one there, you know, I liked seeing the ladies and moving around a little. Lately I just don’t want to go.\n\nAnd there are these little galleries I used to poke into when I had time, just to look around, not buy anything, because who has wall space anymore… but I haven’t felt like doing that either. Even cooking Sunday food for my family, which usually makes me feel like myself, I just… don’t really want to do it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really been watching the scale, to be honest. My eating’s been strange though. Like I’ll have coffee and maybe a few bites of something, and somehow that carries me most of the day.\n\nThen at night I’ll be standing in the kitchen picking at tortillas or some sweets, not even because I want them, just… they’re there. My mother used to say if you’re going to eat, sit down and eat properly, but I don’t really do that lately. I don’t know if my weight changed much, but my appetite’s definitely off.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: Sleep… I’m not sure. I mean, I go to bed, and some nights I’m looking at the ceiling thinking about things, but then again my neighbor’s dog starts up sometimes, and once that happens I’m listening for every little noise.\n\nI can’t really say if it’s a “sleep problem” or just me not settling down right. I haven’t been keeping track of it or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things that happen or things you've done?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do get that feeling. Mostly with the building, the residents. I keep thinking I talked too big, like I promised things before I really checked the money right, and now I feel like I let everybody down.\n\nNobody’s come and said, “You ruined it,” or anything like that, but I still keep turning it over in my head. It’s silly maybe, because you know how people complain about the hall lights or the gate and then next week it’s the parking spaces… but I still feel bad, like I should’ve known better.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy or find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Some things, not so much lately. I used to like going to my dance class, even if I wasn’t the best one there, you know, those ladies can really move. And I liked walking around those little galleries, just looking, not buying anything.\n\nAnd Sundays… I used to cook proper food for my family, the way my mother did, with enough for whoever showed up. Lately I just don’t feel like doing any of that. Those were the things that made me feel like myself, and I’ve kind of been avoiding them.\n\nDoctor: Are there any small pleasures in life that you still find enjoyable?\n\nPatient: Honestly, not many right now. I mean, I still notice little things, like if the coffee comes out good or the sun hits the kitchen in the morning, but the things I usually enjoy… I haven’t really wanted them.\n\nLike my dance class, I keep telling myself I’ll go next week, and then I don’t. And those little galleries I used to wander through, just to look at the paintings and the pottery and all that, I haven’t felt like doing it. Even Sunday cooking for my family… that used to make me feel like me, you know? Now it just feels like too much, and I end up not doing it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel tired or lacking in energy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… tired, yes, sometimes, but I’m not sure if it’s the kind you mean. I’m 64, so some days I sit down and think, well, maybe that’s just my body telling me to slow down.\n\nBut then other days I can get up and do things around the building if I have to. You know, there’s always something — someone saying the elevator smells funny, or the mailbox key isn’t right — so I do move around. I just don’t always feel like I have that… push. I’m not sure if that counts as low energy or just being worn out.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about “significant.” I haven’t really been checking the scale like that. My eating has been strange, though. Some days it’s just coffee and a few bites of something, and that carries me most of the day.\n\nThen later at night I’ll pick at tortillas or something sweet, but it’s not like I’m enjoying it much. It’s more just… there in the kitchen, and I take a little, then walk away. My mother would have told me that’s no way to eat, and she’d be right.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed your clothes fitting looser than they used to?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little, but I haven’t really been paying close attention to the clothes part. I wear a lot of loose things around the apartment anyway, you know, because I’m going up and down stairs or dealing with people in the building.\n\nBut my eating has definitely been off. Like I said, sometimes coffee and a few bites of something will carry me most of the day, and then at night I’ll pick at tortillas or something sweet, not because I’m really enjoying it, just because it’s there. So… maybe that would make things fit different, I’m just not sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost more than 5% of your body weight in the past month?\n\nPatient: I couldn’t say 5%, doctor. I don’t really keep track like that, and the scale… half the time I forget it’s even there.\n\nI just know my eating’s been off. Like coffee and a few bites of something will get me through most of the day, and then at night I’ll pick at tortillas or something sweet, but I’m not really enjoying it. It’s more like I wander into the kitchen, take a little, then go back to whatever I was doing. So maybe I’ve lost some, but I don’t know the number.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you need to tighten your belt or wear smaller sizes because your clothes are too loose?\n\nPatient: Not really with belts or smaller sizes—I don’t use a belt much, and my clothes aren’t the fitted kind anyway. I’ve mostly noticed the eating part, like I’ll have coffee and maybe a few bites of something and that somehow gets me through most of the day.\n\nThen at night I’ll go into the kitchen and pick at a tortilla or something sweet, but it’s not like I’m sitting down to a meal and enjoying it. It’s just there, so I take a little. So maybe clothes would be looser, I don’t know… I just haven’t paid attention to sizes like that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I haven’t really kept track of the weight part, like numbers on a scale. Over the past few months, what I’ve noticed more is my eating has gotten strange.

Some days coffee and just a few bites of something will get me through most of the day, and then later at night I’ll pick at a tortilla or something sweet. Not even because I’m enjoying it, more like I see it there and take a little. Then I get distracted with something in the apartment or the building, and I don’t think about a real meal. So maybe my weight’s changed some, but I couldn’t tell you how much.

Even cooking Sunday food for my family, which usually makes me feel like myself, I just… don’t really want to do it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really been watching the scale, to be honest. My eating’s been strange though. Like I’ll have coffee and maybe a few bites of something, and somehow that carries me most of the day.\n\nThen at night I’ll be standing in the kitchen picking at tortillas or some sweets, not even because I want them, just… they’re there. My mother used to say if you’re going to eat, sit down and eat properly, but I don’t really do that lately. I don’t know if my weight changed much, but my appetite’s definitely off.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: Sleep… I’m not sure. I mean, I go to bed, and some nights I’m looking at the ceiling thinking about things, but then again my neighbor’s dog starts up sometimes, and once that happens I’m listening for every little noise.\n\nI can’t really say if it’s a “sleep problem” or just me not settling down right. I haven’t been keeping track of it or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things that happen or things you've done?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do get that feeling. It’s silly maybe, because you know how people complain about the hall lights or the gate and then next week it’s the parking spaces… but I still feel bad, like I should’ve known better.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy or find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Some things, not so much lately. I used to like going to my dance class, even if I wasn’t the best one there, you know, those ladies can really move. I’m not sure if that counts as low energy or just being worn out.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about “significant.” I haven’t really been checking the scale like that. Noticeable… I mean, maybe? I haven’t been watching the scale. I keep meaning to, and then I get caught up with little things around the building or paperwork and forget.

My eating’s been strange though. I don’t know if my weight changed much, but my appetite’s definitely off.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: Sleep… I’m not sure. I mean, I go to bed, and some nights I’m looking at the ceiling thinking about things, but then again my neighbor’s dog starts up sometimes, and once that happens I’m listening for every little noise.\n\nI can’t really say if it’s a “sleep problem” or just me not settling down right. I haven’t been keeping track of it or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things that happen or things you've done?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do get that feeling. I used to like going to my dance class, even if I wasn’t the best one there, you know, those ladies can really move. No one’s really said, “You’ve lost weight,” not like that. I don’t see people that close anyway—well, I see people in the building, but they’re usually complaining about the parking or the trash bins, so it’s not like they’re looking at me.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I came in because my head just feels… cloudy. Like at work, I’ll be looking at a board packet or some vendor email, and I just sit there staring at it. I used to make those little decisions fast, you know, like which thing needs signing or who to call back, and now even simple things feel hard to sort out.  \n\nAnd it’s funny, because I’ve been doing property stuff a long time—people think it’s just keys and complaints, but it’s a lot of papers—and lately I just can’t get my mind to click into place.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: Sad… I don’t know if I’d call it that exactly. I mean, I get frustrated, sure, especially when I can’t keep up with things the way I used to. But sad like crying all day? Not really. \n\nIt’s more like I’ll sit there and feel stuck, and then I start thinking about all the paperwork piling up, and then that reminds me of this one board president who used to call three times a day about the dumbest things… sorry, I’m getting off track. I guess I haven’t really noticed feeling sad, just… off.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things you used to like?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I haven’t been enjoying them like I used to. Like my dance class—I used to look forward to that, even if I wasn’t the best one there, you know, I liked seeing the ladies and moving around a little. Lately I just don’t want to go.\n\nAnd there are these little galleries I used to poke into when I had time, just to look around, not buy anything, because who has wall space anymore… but I haven’t felt like doing that either. Even cooking Sunday food for my family, which usually makes me feel like myself, I just… don’t really want to do it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really been watching the scale, to be honest. My eating’s been strange though. Like I’ll have coffee and maybe a few bites of something, and somehow that carries me most of the day.\n\nThen at night I’ll be standing in the kitchen picking at tortillas or some sweets, not even because I want them, just… they’re there. My mother used to say if you’re going to eat, sit down and eat properly, but I don’t really do that lately. I don’t know if my weight changed much, but my appetite’s definitely off.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: Sleep… I’m not sure. I mean, I go to bed, and some nights I’m looking at the ceiling thinking about things, but then again my neighbor’s dog starts up sometimes, and once that happens I’m listening for every little noise.\n\nI can’t really say if it’s a “sleep problem” or just me not settling down right. I haven’t been keeping track of it or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things that happen or things you've done?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do get that feeling. Mostly with the building, the residents. I keep thinking I talked too big, like I promised things before I really checked the money right, and now I feel like I let everybody down.\n\nNobody’s come and said, “You ruined it,” or anything like that, but I still keep turning it over in my head. It’s silly maybe, because you know how people complain about the hall lights or the gate and then next week it’s the parking spaces… but I still feel bad, like I should’ve known better.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy or find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Some things, not so much lately. I used to like going to my dance class, even if I wasn’t the best one there, you know, those ladies can really move. And I liked walking around those little galleries, just looking, not buying anything.\n\nAnd Sundays… I used to cook proper food for my family, the way my mother did, with enough for whoever showed up. Lately I just don’t feel like doing any of that. Those were the things that made me feel like myself, and I’ve kind of been avoiding them.\n\nDoctor: Are there any small pleasures in life that you still find enjoyable?\n\nPatient: Honestly, not many right now. I mean, I still notice little things, like if the coffee comes out good or the sun hits the kitchen in the morning, but the things I usually enjoy… I haven’t really wanted them.\n\nLike my dance class, I keep telling myself I’ll go next week, and then I don’t. And those little galleries I used to wander through, just to look at the paintings and the pottery and all that, I haven’t felt like doing it. Even Sunday cooking for my family… that used to make me feel like me, you know? Now it just feels like too much, and I end up not doing it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel tired or lacking in energy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… tired, yes, sometimes, but I’m not sure if it’s the kind you mean. I’m 64, so some days I sit down and think, well, maybe that’s just my body telling me to slow down.\n\nBut then other days I can get up and do things around the building if I have to. You know, there’s always something — someone saying the elevator smells funny, or the mailbox key isn’t right — so I do move around. I just don’t always feel like I have that… push. I’m not sure if that counts as low energy or just being worn out.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about “significant.” I haven’t really been checking the scale like that. My eating has been strange, though. Some days it’s just coffee and a few bites of something, and that carries me most of the day.\n\nThen later at night I’ll pick at tortillas or something sweet, but it’s not like I’m enjoying it much. It’s more just… there in the kitchen, and I take a little, then walk away. My mother would have told me that’s no way to eat, and she’d be right.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed your clothes fitting looser than they used to?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little, but I haven’t really been paying close attention to the clothes part. I wear a lot of loose things around the apartment anyway, you know, because I’m going up and down stairs or dealing with people in the building.\n\nBut my eating has definitely been off. Like I said, sometimes coffee and a few bites of something will carry me most of the day, and then at night I’ll pick at tortillas or something sweet, not because I’m really enjoying it, just because it’s there. So… maybe that would make things fit different, I’m just not sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost more than 5% of your body weight in the past month?\n\nPatient: I couldn’t say 5%, doctor. I don’t really keep track like that, and the scale… half the time I forget it’s even there.\n\nI just know my eating’s been off. Like coffee and a few bites of something will get me through most of the day, and then at night I’ll pick at tortillas or something sweet, but I’m not really enjoying it. It’s more like I wander into the kitchen, take a little, then go back to whatever I was doing. So maybe I’ve lost some, but I don’t know the number.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you need to tighten your belt or wear smaller sizes because your clothes are too loose?\n\nPatient: Not really with belts or smaller sizes—I don’t use a belt much, and my clothes aren’t the fitted kind anyway. It’s just there, so I take a little. So maybe clothes would be looser, I don’t know… I just haven’t paid attention to sizes like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really kept track of the weight part, like numbers on a scale. Over the past few months, what I’ve noticed more is my eating has gotten strange.\n\nSome days coffee and just a few bites of something will get me through most of the day, and then later at night I’ll pick at a tortilla or something sweet. Not even because I’m enjoying it, more like I see it there and take a little. Then I get distracted with something in the apartment or the building, and I don’t think about a real meal. So maybe my weight’s changed some, but I couldn’t tell you how much.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a noticeable amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Noticeable… I mean, maybe? Even Sunday cooking for my family… that used to make me feel like me, you know? Now it just feels like too much, and I end up not doing it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel tired or lacking in energy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… tired, yes, sometimes, but I’m not sure if it’s the kind you mean. I’m 64, so some days I sit down and think, well, maybe that’s just my body telling me to slow down.\n\nBut then other days I can get up and do things around the building if I have to. You know, there’s always something — someone saying the elevator smells funny, or the mailbox key isn’t right — so I do move around. I just don’t always feel like I have that… push. I’m not sure if that counts as low energy or just being worn out.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about “significant.” I haven’t really been checking the scale like that. My eating has been strange, though. Some days it’s just coffee and a few bites of something, and that carries me most of the day.\n\nThen later at night I’ll pick at tortillas or something sweet, but it’s not like I’m enjoying it much. It’s more just… there in the kitchen, and I take a little, then walk away. My mother would have told me that’s no way to eat, and she’d be right.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed your clothes fitting looser than they used to?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little, but I haven’t really been paying close attention to the clothes part. I wear a lot of loose things around the apartment anyway, you know, because I’m going up and down stairs or dealing with people in the building.\n\nBut my eating has definitely been off. Like I said, sometimes coffee and a few bites of something will carry me most of the day, and then at night I’ll pick at tortillas or something sweet, not because I’m really enjoying it, just because it’s there. So… maybe that would make things fit different, I’m just not sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost more than 5% of your body weight in the past month?\n\nPatient: I couldn’t say 5%, doctor. I don’t really keep track like that, and the scale… half the time I forget it’s even there.\n\nI just know my eating’s been off. Like coffee and a few bites of something will get me through most of the day, and then at night I’ll pick at tortillas or something sweet, but I’m not really enjoying it. It’s more like I wander into the kitchen, take a little, then go back to whatever I was doing. So maybe I’ve lost some, but I don’t know the number.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you need to tighten your belt or wear smaller sizes because your clothes are too loose?\n\nPatient: Not really with belts or smaller sizes—I don’t use a belt much, and my clothes aren’t the fitted kind anyway. I’ve mostly noticed the eating part, like I’ll have coffee and maybe a few bites of something and that somehow gets me through most of the day.\n\nThen at night I’ll go into the kitchen and pick at a tortilla or something sweet, but it’s not like I’m sitting down to a meal and enjoying it. It’s just there, so I take a little. So maybe clothes would be looser, I don’t know… I just haven’t paid attention to sizes like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really kept track of the weight part, like numbers on a scale. So maybe my weight’s changed some, but I couldn’t tell you how much.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a noticeable amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Noticeable… I mean, maybe? Coffee and a few bites of something can carry me most of the day, and then later I’ll just pick at a tortilla or something sweet, not even because it tastes good. Just… it’s there, I take a little, and then I’m off doing something else. So if I’ve lost weight, it’s probably from that, but I can’t tell you how much.\n\nDoctor: Have you had anyone comment on your weight loss recently?\n\nPatient: No one’s really said, “You’ve lost weight,” not like that. I don’t see people that close anyway—well, I see people in the building, but they’re usually complaining about the parking or the trash bins, so it’s not like they’re looking at me.\n\nI just know my eating’s been off. Coffee and a few bites will take me through most of the day, and then at night I’ll pick at a tortilla or something sweet, but I’m not really enjoying it. It’s just… there, and I take a little. So maybe someone could notice, but nobody’s said it to me.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone noticed or commented that you appear thinner than you used to be?\n\nPatient: Not really, no one’s come out and said I look thinner. I mean, people notice everything in my building—who left boxes by the bins, whose car is in the wrong spot—but nobody’s said that to me.\n\nWhat I can say is my eating’s been off. Coffee and a few bites of something can get me through most of the day, and then at night I’ll pick at a tortilla or something sweet, but I’m not really enjoying it. It’s just kind of… there, so I take a little.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost weight unintentionally?\n\nPatient: I don’t know for sure if I’ve lost weight, not on purpose or anything. I just haven’t been eating like I should. Coffee gets me through most of the day with maybe a few bites of something, and then later I’ll pick at a tortilla or something sweet… not because I’m hungry exactly, just because it’s sitting there.\n\nAnd then I get distracted, like I’ll remember I need to call someone about the hallway light or check a notice, and the food just… doesn’t really matter. So if I did lose weight, it would be from that, but I haven’t been keeping track.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you lost weight without trying to?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 64 / property_real_estate_or_community_association_manager / less_than_9th\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Weight_Appetite_Change\nmanifestation: \"My eating has been off, where coffee and a few bites carry me most of the day and then at night I pick at tortillas or sweets without really enjoying them.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I haven’t been trying to, no. I don’t really know if the number’s changed, I don’t weigh myself much, but my eating’s been… off. Like coffee and a few bites will carry me through most of the day.

Then at night I’ll pick at a tortilla or something sweet, but it’s not like I’m sitting down and enjoying a meal. It’s just there, and I take a little. So if I lost weight, it wasn’t on purpose.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I came in because my head just feels… cloudy. Like at work, I’ll be looking at a board packet or some vendor email, and I just sit there staring at it. I used to make those little decisions fast, you know, like which thing needs signing or who to call back, and now even simple things feel hard to sort out.  \n\nAnd it’s funny, because I’ve been doing property stuff a long time—people think it’s just keys and complaints, but it’s a lot of papers—and lately I just can’t get my mind to click into place.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: Sad… I don’t know if I’d call it that exactly. I mean, I get frustrated, sure, especially when I can’t keep up with things the way I used to. But sad like crying all day? Not really. \n\nIt’s more like I’ll sit there and feel stuck, and then I start thinking about all the paperwork piling up, and then that reminds me of this one board president who used to call three times a day about the dumbest things… sorry, I’m getting off track. I guess I haven’t really noticed feeling sad, just… off.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things you used to like?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I haven’t been enjoying them like I used to. Like my dance class—I used to look forward to that, even if I wasn’t the best one there, you know, I liked seeing the ladies and moving around a little. Lately I just don’t want to go.\n\nAnd there are these little galleries I used to poke into when I had time, just to look around, not buy anything, because who has wall space anymore… but I haven’t felt like doing that either. Even cooking Sunday food for my family, which usually makes me feel like myself, I just… don’t really want to do it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really been watching the scale, to be honest. My eating’s been strange though. Like I’ll have coffee and maybe a few bites of something, and somehow that carries me most of the day.\n\nThen at night I’ll be standing in the kitchen picking at tortillas or some sweets, not even because I want them, just… they’re there. My mother used to say if you’re going to eat, sit down and eat properly, but I don’t really do that lately. I don’t know if my weight changed much, but my appetite’s definitely off.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: Sleep… I’m not sure. I mean, I go to bed, and some nights I’m looking at the ceiling thinking about things, but then again my neighbor’s dog starts up sometimes, and once that happens I’m listening for every little noise.\n\nI can’t really say if it’s a “sleep problem” or just me not settling down right. I haven’t been keeping track of it or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things that happen or things you've done?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do get that feeling. Mostly with the building, the residents. I keep thinking I talked too big, like I promised things before I really checked the money right, and now I feel like I let everybody down.\n\nNobody’s come and said, “You ruined it,” or anything like that, but I still keep turning it over in my head. It’s silly maybe, because you know how people complain about the hall lights or the gate and then next week it’s the parking spaces… but I still feel bad, like I should’ve known better.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy or find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Some things, not so much lately. I used to like going to my dance class, even if I wasn’t the best one there, you know, those ladies can really move. And I liked walking around those little galleries, just looking, not buying anything.\n\nAnd Sundays… I used to cook proper food for my family, the way my mother did, with enough for whoever showed up. Lately I just don’t feel like doing any of that. Those were the things that made me feel like myself, and I’ve kind of been avoiding them.\n\nDoctor: Are there any small pleasures in life that you still find enjoyable?\n\nPatient: Honestly, not many right now. I mean, I still notice little things, like if the coffee comes out good or the sun hits the kitchen in the morning, but the things I usually enjoy… I haven’t really wanted them.\n\nLike my dance class, I keep telling myself I’ll go next week, and then I don’t. And those little galleries I used to wander through, just to look at the paintings and the pottery and all that, I haven’t felt like doing it. Even Sunday cooking for my family… that used to make me feel like me, you know? Now it just feels like too much, and I end up not doing it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel tired or lacking in energy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… tired, yes, sometimes, but I’m not sure if it’s the kind you mean. I’m 64, so some days I sit down and think, well, maybe that’s just my body telling me to slow down.\n\nBut then other days I can get up and do things around the building if I have to. You know, there’s always something — someone saying the elevator smells funny, or the mailbox key isn’t right — so I do move around. I just don’t always feel like I have that… push. I’m not sure if that counts as low energy or just being worn out.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about “significant.” I haven’t really been checking the scale like that. My eating has been strange, though. Some days it’s just coffee and a few bites of something, and that carries me most of the day.\n\nThen later at night I’ll pick at tortillas or something sweet, but it’s not like I’m enjoying it much. It’s more just… there in the kitchen, and I take a little, then walk away. My mother would have told me that’s no way to eat, and she’d be right.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed your clothes fitting looser than they used to?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little, but I haven’t really been paying close attention to the clothes part. I wear a lot of loose things around the apartment anyway, you know, because I’m going up and down stairs or dealing with people in the building.\n\nBut my eating has definitely been off. Like I said, sometimes coffee and a few bites of something will carry me most of the day, and then at night I’ll pick at tortillas or something sweet, not because I’m really enjoying it, just because it’s there. So… maybe that would make things fit different, I’m just not sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost more than 5% of your body weight in the past month?\n\nPatient: I couldn’t say 5%, doctor. I don’t really keep track like that, and the scale… half the time I forget it’s even there.\n\nI just know my eating’s been off. Like coffee and a few bites of something will get me through most of the day, and then at night I’ll pick at tortillas or something sweet, but I’m not really enjoying it. It’s more like I wander into the kitchen, take a little, then go back to whatever I was doing. So maybe I’ve lost some, but I don’t know the number.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you need to tighten your belt or wear smaller sizes because your clothes are too loose?\n\nPatient: Not really with belts or smaller sizes—I don’t use a belt much, and my clothes aren’t the fitted kind anyway. I’ve mostly noticed the eating part, like I’ll have coffee and maybe a few bites of something and that somehow gets me through most of the day.\n\nThen at night I’ll go into the kitchen and pick at a tortilla or something sweet, but it’s not like I’m sitting down to a meal and enjoying it. It’s just there, so I take a little. So maybe clothes would be looser, I don’t know… I just haven’t paid attention to sizes like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really kept track of the weight part, like numbers on a scale. Over the past few months, what I’ve noticed more is my eating has gotten strange.\n\nSome days coffee and just a few bites of something will get me through most of the day, and then later at night I’ll pick at a tortilla or something sweet. Not even because I’m enjoying it, more like I see it there and take a little. Then I get distracted with something in the apartment or the building, and I don’t think about a real meal. So maybe my weight’s changed some, but I couldn’t tell you how much.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a noticeable amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Noticeable… I mean, maybe? Coffee and a few bites will take me through most of the day, and then at night I’ll pick at a tortilla or something sweet, but I’m not really enjoying it. It’s just… there, and I take a little. Like I’ll have coffee and maybe a few bites of something, and somehow that carries me most of the day.\n\nThen at night I’ll be standing in the kitchen picking at tortillas or some sweets, not even because I want them, just… they’re there. My mother used to say if you’re going to eat, sit down and eat properly, but I don’t really do that lately. I don’t know if my weight changed much, but my appetite’s definitely off.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: Sleep… I’m not sure. I mean, I go to bed, and some nights I’m looking at the ceiling thinking about things, but then again my neighbor’s dog starts up sometimes, and once that happens I’m listening for every little noise.\n\nI can’t really say if it’s a “sleep problem” or just me not settling down right. I haven’t been keeping track of it or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things that happen or things you've done?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do get that feeling. Mostly with the building, the residents. I keep thinking I talked too big, like I promised things before I really checked the money right, and now I feel like I let everybody down.\n\nNobody’s come and said, “You ruined it,” or anything like that, but I still keep turning it over in my head. It’s silly maybe, because you know how people complain about the hall lights or the gate and then next week it’s the parking spaces… but I still feel bad, like I should’ve known better.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy or find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Some things, not so much lately. I used to like going to my dance class, even if I wasn’t the best one there, you know, those ladies can really move. And I liked walking around those little galleries, just looking, not buying anything.\n\nAnd Sundays… I used to cook proper food for my family, the way my mother did, with enough for whoever showed up. Lately I just don’t feel like doing any of that. Those were the things that made me feel like myself, and I’ve kind of been avoiding them.\n\nDoctor: Are there any small pleasures in life that you still find enjoyable?\n\nPatient: Honestly, not many right now. I mean, I still notice little things, like if the coffee comes out good or the sun hits the kitchen in the morning, but the things I usually enjoy… I haven’t really wanted them.\n\nLike my dance class, I keep telling myself I’ll go next week, and then I don’t. And those little galleries I used to wander through, just to look at the paintings and the pottery and all that, I haven’t felt like doing it. Even Sunday cooking for my family… that used to make me feel like me, you know? Now it just feels like too much, and I end up not doing it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel tired or lacking in energy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… tired, yes, sometimes, but I’m not sure if it’s the kind you mean. I’m 64, so some days I sit down and think, well, maybe that’s just my body telling me to slow down.\n\nBut then other days I can get up and do things around the building if I have to. You know, there’s always something — someone saying the elevator smells funny, or the mailbox key isn’t right — so I do move around. I just don’t always feel like I have that… push. I’m not sure if that counts as low energy or just being worn out.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about “significant.” I haven’t really been checking the scale like that. My eating has been strange, though. Some days it’s just coffee and a few bites of something, and that carries me most of the day.\n\nThen later at night I’ll pick at tortillas or something sweet, but it’s not like I’m enjoying it much. It’s more just… there in the kitchen, and I take a little, then walk away. My mother would have told me that’s no way to eat, and she’d be right.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed your clothes fitting looser than they used to?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little, but I haven’t really been paying close attention to the clothes part. I wear a lot of loose things around the apartment anyway, you know, because I’m going up and down stairs or dealing with people in the building.\n\nBut my eating has definitely been off. Like I said, sometimes coffee and a few bites of something will carry me most of the day, and then at night I’ll pick at tortillas or something sweet, not because I’m really enjoying it, just because it’s there. So… maybe that would make things fit different, I’m just not sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost more than 5% of your body weight in the past month?\n\nPatient: I couldn’t say 5%, doctor. I don’t really keep track like that, and the scale… half the time I forget it’s even there.\n\nI just know my eating’s been off. Like coffee and a few bites of something will get me through most of the day, and then at night I’ll pick at tortillas or something sweet, but I’m not really enjoying it. It’s more like I wander into the kitchen, take a little, then go back to whatever I was doing. So maybe I’ve lost some, but I don’t know the number.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you need to tighten your belt or wear smaller sizes because your clothes are too loose?\n\nPatient: Not really with belts or smaller sizes—I don’t use a belt much, and my clothes aren’t the fitted kind anyway. I’ve mostly noticed the eating part, like I’ll have coffee and maybe a few bites of something and that somehow gets me through most of the day.\n\nThen at night I’ll go into the kitchen and pick at a tortilla or something sweet, but it’s not like I’m sitting down to a meal and enjoying it. It’s just there, so I take a little. So maybe clothes would be looser, I don’t know… I just haven’t paid attention to sizes like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really kept track of the weight part, like numbers on a scale. Over the past few months, what I’ve noticed more is my eating has gotten strange.\n\nSome days coffee and just a few bites of something will get me through most of the day, and then later at night I’ll pick at a tortilla or something sweet. Not even because I’m enjoying it, more like I see it there and take a little. Then I get distracted with something in the apartment or the building, and I don’t think about a real meal. So maybe my weight’s changed some, but I couldn’t tell you how much.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a noticeable amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Noticeable… I mean, maybe? I haven’t been watching the scale. I keep meaning to, and then I get caught up with little things around the building or paperwork and forget.\n\nWhat I do notice is I’m not eating right. Coffee and a few bites of something can carry me most of the day, and then later I’ll just pick at a tortilla or something sweet, not even because it tastes good. Just… it’s there, I take a little, and then I’m off doing something else. So if I’ve lost weight, it’s probably from that, but I can’t tell you how much.\n\nDoctor: Have you had anyone comment on your weight loss recently?\n\nPatient: No one’s really said, “You’ve lost weight,” not like that. I don’t see people that close anyway—well, I see people in the building, but they’re usually complaining about the parking or the trash bins, so it’s not like they’re looking at me.\n\nI just know my eating’s been off. Coffee and a few bites will take me through most of the day, and then at night I’ll pick at a tortilla or something sweet, but I’m not really enjoying it. It’s just… there, and I take a little. So maybe someone could notice, but nobody’s said it to me.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone noticed or commented that you appear thinner than you used to be?\n\nPatient: Not really, no one’s come out and said I look thinner. I mean, people notice everything in my building—who left boxes by the bins, whose car is in the wrong spot—but nobody’s said that to me.\n\nWhat I can say is my eating’s been off. Coffee and a few bites of something can get me through most of the day, and then at night I’ll pick at a tortilla or something sweet, but I’m not really enjoying it. It’s just kind of… there, so I take a little.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost weight unintentionally?\n\nPatient: I don’t know for sure if I’ve lost weight, not on purpose or anything. I just haven’t been eating like I should. Coffee gets me through most of the day with maybe a few bites of something, and then later I’ll pick at a tortilla or something sweet… not because I’m hungry exactly, just because it’s sitting there.\n\nAnd then I get distracted, like I’ll remember I need to call someone about the hallway light or check a notice, and the food just… doesn’t really matter. So if I did lose weight, it would be from that, but I haven’t been keeping track.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost weight without trying to?\n\nPatient: I haven’t been trying to, no. I don’t really know if the number’s changed, I don’t weigh myself much, but my eating’s been… off. Like coffee and a few bites will carry me through most of the day.\n\nThen at night I’ll pick at a tortilla or something sweet, but it’s not like I’m sitting down and enjoying a meal. It’s just there, and I take a little. So if I lost weight, it wasn’t on purpose.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a significant weight loss without trying to diet?\n\nPatient: Not dieting, no. I’m not doing one of those plans or anything—I never had much patience for that, and anyway the ladies in my building are always talking about points and carbs and I just tune out.\n\nBut my eating has been off. Coffee and a few bites of something will carry me most of the day, and then at night I’ll pick at a tortilla or something sweet. I’m not really enjoying it, it’s more like it’s there, so I have a little. So if weight came off, it wasn’t because I was trying."
